| M3-04 | [ ] Prove MCP session isolation, concurrent request correlation, explicit unknown outcomes, and no replay of side effects. | implemented awaiting verification | mcp | **Product change.** `mcp-2025-11-25` sessions are now bound to the authenticated principal. The relay ingress — the only endpoint that verified the consumer credential — derives an opaque `tunnel-principal-binding` (a one-way SHA-256 over a versioned domain separator and the tenant, principal, device and service, truncated to 128 bits), allowlisted by the session profile only; a consumer that sends the header itself is refused `400 HTTP_INVALID_HEAD` before anything is forwarded, so the value is never taken from the request. Both export kinds require exactly the binding a session was opened with on POST, GET and DELETE, and answer any other with the unknown-session 404 byte for byte. See [mcp.md](mcp.md#pinned-in-code-m3-04) for the full design and its rationale.<br><br>**Evidence.** New harness gate `verify-m3-mcp-isolation` (registered in `scripts/m3-harness-verify.sh`, documented in [testing.md](testing.md#mcp-isolation-correlation-and-unknown-outcomes-verify-m3-mcp-isolation)) on the real three-relay cluster with two distinct authenticated principals plus a third whose grant is revoked: three consecutive standalone runs exited 0. Session isolation (foreign POST/GET/DELETE 404, byte-identical to an unknown session, both sessions unaffected, 4/4 notifications with no cross-delivery, 2 sessions and 2 children); correlation (24 concurrent answers across colliding JSON-RPC IDs and progress tokens in both profiles, 0 misrouted, duplicates refused 400); revocation (refused in ~10 ms with `404 SERVICE_NOT_FOUND` `not_dispatched`, admitted exchange withdrawn in ~500 ms with `502 HTTP_STREAM_INTERRUPTED` `unknown`, 0 dispatched after, siblings and device session untouched); one call across 3 completed rotations answered exactly once with 1 dispatch; lost acknowledgement and owner process loss each `outcome_unknown` with the side effect recorded exactly once. Unit tests: `tunnel-mcp` profile allowlist; `tunnel-relay` binding derivation, the ingress refusal of a consumer-supplied binding (every spelling and a repeat), and the owner's re-derivation and comparison; `tunnel-mcp-fixture` `principal_binding` for both export kinds, `session_lifecycle` export shutdown, and the Streamable HTTP capacity policy. Red-then-green for the binding check and for the revocation assertions is recorded in [testing.md](testing.md#mcp-isolation-correlation-and-unknown-outcomes-verify-m3-mcp-isolation).<br><br>**Review round 2.** The Streamable HTTP export's session table now expires entries unused for its own `session_idle_seconds` (new `streamable-http` setting, same default and bounds as the stdio backend), so a client that crashes and restarts without a DELETE no longer leaks a slot per restart; the capacity refusal is counted in `rejected`; `HttpHandlers` is no longer `Clone`, because its `Drop` ends every session its exports hold; the owner compares the binding header name ASCII-case-insensitively, as the codec allowlist does; and the gate's OPEN journal bound is derived as `CORRELATION_CALLS * 4` (two adjacent bursts fully unreclaimed) rather than tuned. The case-insensitive comparison is defence in depth rather than a fix: `http-forward/1` header names must be lowercase (`validate_header_name`/`is_tchar_lower`), so a differently-spelled name is refused by the codec before the owner compares anything, and the review's cited `eq_ignore_ascii_case` is the query-parameter credential check, not a header-name rule.<br><br>**Remaining before verified.** Server→client JSON-RPC request IDs are unproven (the fixture issues none; M3-13), the Streamable HTTP backend's binding is proven only through the in-process bridge, cross-tenant consumers are not driven here, and `Origin`/OAuth remain M3-11. The full `scripts/m3-harness-verify.sh` and workspace suites must be rerun at the merged revision.<br><br>**Rerun at `db1da30` on 2026-09-16: not promotable.** `verify-m3-mcp-isolation` failed 2 of 17 runs at that revision - 1 of the 3 inside `scripts/m3-harness-verify.sh` and 1 of 14 standalone - and 2 of 10 more at the follow-up commit that added the diagnostic below, so the gate is not reliably green and this row stays implemented awaiting verification. Every failure is the same shape: the `unknown-outcome` case reaches `owner_to_ingress_path_blackholed` at about 20.8 s and the side-effect marker never appears inside the 30 s wait. Passing runs take 33-39 s, failing ones 53-55 s.<br><br>**What the failures show, and what they do not.** The first pass asserted a cause it had not established; that claim is withdrawn. The gate aborted the consumer's call on this timeout and discarded its response, so nothing recorded what the consumer saw. `unknown_outcome` now gives that call a bounded 15 s grace and names its answer before failing - failure path only, asserting nothing and unable to turn a failing run green. With it, both reproductions report `consumer status 503 code="PEER_UNAVAILABLE" execution="unknown"`. That narrows the cause without closing it. In `tunnel-relay/src/http.rs` the `PEER_UNTRUSTED` `not_dispatched` arm is raised from the membership snapshot, which a failed-closed publication does not touch, so the answer is the transport-failure arm instead.  That arm covers both a hop closed mid-request and a fresh dial refused because the transport pin set (`SharedPeerPins`, the verifier the dial consumes) was empty, and neither is established.  The consumer's POST also stayed outstanding for about 30 s before the 503, which fits a handshake or idle timeout better than an immediate pin-watcher close and is itself a data point for that correlation. `execution="unknown"` means the ingress genuinely cannot tell whether the owner was reached, which is consistent with the side effect never running. Settling it needs owner-side correlation for that exchange, not the consumer's status alone.<br><br>**Which row owns it.** The symptom - a peer stream dying at a membership re-sign - is what **M7-C80** describes and [cluster.md](cluster.md) already records as a known interruption, reached here through the failed-closed pin publication **M7-C83** covers. Both are planned and stay planned. The earlier reading that peer readiness failed to recover is contradicted by the logs: each failing run carries exactly one `production membership pin publication failed closed` warning and **zero** `peer readiness probe failed` warnings, while passing runs carry two of each. Nor is this fixture-only - `tunnel-relay/src/main.rs`'s invalidation callback clears the relay's pins on a failed-closed publication and recovers only on the next refresh tick, the same shape as the fixture's; the fixture-specific part was the permanent loss M7-C81 already fixed.<br><br>**Unchanged gaps.** The Streamable HTTP backend's binding is still proven only through the in-process bridge, cross-tenant consumers are still not driven, server&rarr;client request IDs stay in M3-13 and `Origin`/OAuth stays in M3-11.<br><br>**Cause settled on 2026-09-16 at `1fb2d99`, with owner-side correlation.** The gate's unknown-outcome failure path now joins the consumer's answer with every relay's own bounded `role/stage/cause` fault tuples, its transport pin state, and the consumer's latency measured inside the request task. Three reproductions, identical in shape, say this: the ingress relay-c recorded **exactly one** fault tuple, `ingress/pool_connect/transport_other`, carrying a peer request identifier; **no relay recorded an owner-role tuple at all**, so the owner never saw the stream; and the consumer's POST was answered **12-14 ms** after it was issued. The "roughly 30 s the POST stayed outstanding" recorded above was an artefact of the gate's own 30 s marker wait and is **withdrawn** - the request did not hang.<br><br>**What is and is not probative here.** The stage label alone is not: pre-fix, `pool_connect/transport_other` covered *both* the pre-dial refusal on an empty pin set and a post-dial SPKI or role failure, because both were `PeerTransportError::Authentication`. Neither is the forensic `pins_empty=false`, which is sampled only after the 30 s marker wait, by which time the refresh tick (at most 5 s) has long republished; it says nothing about the failing instant. What actually pins the mechanism is the conjunction: the single co-occurring `membership pin publication failed closed` warning naming `Unready(MembershipRejected)` on that same relay at that same moment, the 12-14 ms latency (too fast for a handshake, a dial or an idle deadline, and consistent only with a refusal taken before any I/O), the total absence of any owner-role tuple, and - decisively - a post-fix run that passed *through* the same transient unready reconcile that produced every failure. Making the pre-dial refusal its own typed error, `PinsUnavailable` with cause `transport_pins_unavailable`, is what removes the ambiguity for any future occurrence. So of the two candidates the earlier reading could not separate, this is **the fresh dial refused on an empty transport pin set**, not an in-flight hop closed mid-request; **M7-C80 is not the owner of this symptom**.<br><br>**Why the pin set was empty, and why no readiness probe failed.** A new warning on the relay's periodic membership reconcile named the upstream trigger: a case-boundary re-sign raced the reconcile, the candidate verifier held no membership for this node at or above the checkpoint's minimum version, and `install_unready_candidate` set the runtime unready and invalidated every admission, returning `PeerRejected`. That site reported `MembershipRejected`, which was inaccurate - every record had verified; what was missing was this relay's *own* usable membership - and it now reports `MissingLocalMembership`, the same reason as a checkpoint that does not name this node at all. The distinction is load-bearing for the fix below. The invalidation callback's pin publication then failed closed and emptied the set. No readiness-probe warning appears because the refresh tick republishes pins *before* it probes, so the probe never ran against an empty set - which is exactly why the earlier reading that peer readiness had failed was wrong.<br><br>**(On branch `m3c10-isolation-flake`, held as M7-C86; not on `main` and not on the gate-only branch.)** **Fixed in the product at `925cf9c`.** Readiness is a statement about this relay's own cluster prerequisites, not about whether the peer keys it already verified are still approved, and `peer_refresh_loop`'s unready branch in `tunnel-relay/src/main.rs` already documented the intended behaviour - keep the verified route and pin set installed and withdraw *readiness* instead - but could never reach it, because `publish_membership_pins` had already emptied the set. **(On branch `m3c10-isolation-flake`, held as M7-C86; not on `main` and not on the gate-only branch.)** **Recorded owner decision (2026-09-16), after review:** the retention is narrowed by *why* the relay is unready, not applied to every unready state. Rejected **trust evidence** - an unverifiable authority, a record that failed verification, an expired checkpoint - still withdraws the pin set and fails closed in both directions; a **local or transient** unready state - this relay's own membership missing from a candidate, a catalog read, persistence, cancellation - retains the verified set and withdraws readiness instead. `MembershipUnreadyReason::withdraws_peer_trust` is that table, with a unit test naming every variant so a new one must choose deliberately. A malformed digest still fails closed in either case. The fixture mirrors the contract exactly.<br><br>**Why the split, and the regression it repairs.** The first attempt retained pins for *every* unready state and broke a registered, verified M7 gate: `m7_deployment_spki_replacement` (`scripts/m7-harness-verify.sh`) failed at phase 6 with `relay A did not reach the expected rejected outcome for the relay-b-replacement certificate before the bounded deadline (last=accepted)`. Under a record signed by an untrusted key, relay A must refuse both the rogue and the replacement certificate on the authenticated probe; retaining pins let it complete mTLS and accept, contradicting [cluster.md](cluster.md) and [testing.md](testing.md). That gate was not in the first rerun set, which is how it was missed. Causation was then established here in both directions: with the retention widened back, the gate fails at phase 6 with that exact message in 65 s; with the split in place it passes in 68 s. Exposure had been bounded - the probe is payload-free and stream admission still failed `NotReady` - but it was verified M7 behaviour changing, and the narrowed split keeps the fail-closed property exactly where it is about an attacker's evidence while still fixing the self-inflicted race. Separately, a dial that does race a genuinely empty pin set now says so in its own right: `PeerTransportError::PinsUnavailable` is distinct from an `Authentication` failure because no socket was opened and no certificate examined, so the ingress answers `not_dispatched` with a bounded retry hint instead of the `unknown` a consumer cannot act on, and records the `transport_pins_unavailable` cause. **Red-then-green:** `verify-m3-mcp-isolation` failed **6 of 24** runs before the change (2 of 8, 1 of 8, 3 of 8), every failure the tuple above; **0 of 8** after, one of those eight passing *through* the same transient unready reconcile. Unit tests `dialling_with_no_published_pins_is_its_own_typed_refusal` (`tunnel-transport`) and `unpublished_peer_trust_is_not_dispatched_and_retryable` (`tunnel-relay`, which also pins that a dialled peer's authentication failure stays `unknown`) cover the two classifications.<br><br>**Both stated gaps closed at `6297458`.** The gate gained two cases, so it now has eight. **`streamable-binding`** drives the principal binding over the real cluster against a **Streamable HTTP** export instead of a stdio one, which is what separates the binding from process isolation: a stdio export gives every session its own child, while a Streamable HTTP backend is one shared process with one session table serving every principal. The case asserts that shape rather than assuming it — no per-session child is spawned, so the refusal is the export's own binding table (`SessionBindings::permits`, which refuses a foreign principal before the backend is dialled) rather than the backend or a process boundary — and checks each session's end by the session becoming unusable rather than by a status, because this backend acknowledges a DELETE with 202 where the stdio export answers 204. **`cross-tenant`** drives `consumer-b-1`, authenticated and genuinely authorized in tenant B, against tenant A's device, service and *live* session: one `initialize` plus that session ID on POST, GET and DELETE, each paired with the same route naming a session that never existed. All seven are refused `404 DEVICE_NOT_FOUND` `not_dispatched` - never `unknown` - each pair indistinguishable, nothing dispatched, no export session opened, the device exchange log unchanged, and tenant A's own session still answered exactly. Red-then-green: with the export's binding comparison disabled, consumer B's POST, GET and DELETE on consumer A's Streamable HTTP session were accepted 200/200/202 and the case failed naming those statuses; with the cross-tenant case driven by a same-tenant principal holding a real grant it failed with `0 of 7 refused, initialize 200`. Every new validator rule is also exercised by a single-mutation unit test. The gate's SSE reader was fixed with them: it treated everything after `data: ` as the payload, which is only correct when `data` is the event's last field, so every message the shared backend sent (which follows it with `id:` and `retry:`) was dropped.<br><br>**Not promoted. Promotion at `6297458` is withdrawn after review.** That promotion cited 12 of 12 standalone runs, and those runs happened; what they did not establish is that the fix handles the condition, because **none of the twelve hit the membership race at all** — zero reconcile failures across all twelve — so they were equally consistent with the race not occurring. Worse, the fix as written at that revision broke a registered, verified M7 gate that was not in the rerun set: `m7_deployment_spki_replacement` phase 6, where a record signed by an untrusted key must make relay A refuse both the rogue and the replacement certificate on the authenticated probe. Retaining pins for *every* unready state let it complete mTLS and accept. See the owner decision below. **Evidence at `85cdd24` on `m3c10-gate-only`, which carries the gate work without the retention.** `verify-m3-mcp-isolation` is **11 of 12** standalone (10 of 12 at the earlier `40ecbdf`; the gate is flaky on `main` at roughly 1 in 6, so neither figure is a clean rate): the blackout this row diagnosed is fixed only by the retention change held back as **M7-C86**, so the flake returns without it, exactly as expected. Both failures are the known shape and are now fully self-diagnosing: one `production membership pin publication failed closed` warning, an `ingress/pool_connect/transport_pins_unavailable` tuple on the ingress, no owner-role tuple, and the consumer answered in 14 ms. The consumer-facing outcome is nevertheless **improved** by what did land: `503 PEER_UNAVAILABLE` with **`execution="not_dispatched"`** and a bounded retry hint, where this row previously recorded `unknown` — a consumer may now act on it. `scripts/m3-harness-verify.sh` passed end to end as the script in 300 s. Workspace evidence at the same revision: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --workspace --locked -- --test-threads=4` with Redis (1314 passed, 0 failed, 52 ignored); `cargo test -p tunnel-test-harness --locked` standalone (327 passed, 0 failed, 12 ignored). All **18** peer trust/membership/readiness gates pass, including `m7_deployment_spki_replacement`; `verify-m7-i08-synthetic-rotation` needed a rerun, failing once of three on an unrelated CLI shutdown-cleanup path (`I08 CLI shutdown returned signal: 9`, the M7-C84 class) and passing the other two. `verify-m7-c11-diagnostics` and `verify-m7-og02-correlation` also pass, which the C11 cause-vocabulary fix made load-bearing. And `verify-m7-trust-expiry` is **not** regressed: measured interleaved against `origin/main` with both binaries pre-built, **8 of 8 on each arm**.<br><br>**With the retention work applied** (M7-C86, unlanded): 12 of 12 standalone runs, the race actually firing about twice per run. The earlier 11-of-12 round is characterised rather than left open: it ran at `4235f5e`, *before* the `MissingLocalKey` split, and its one failure shows an empty pin set, an `ingress/pool_connect/transport_pins_unavailable` refusal and **no pin-publication warning at all** — the signature of the second silent withdrawal site, this relay's own certificate not being an approved key of its own record, which then reported `MembershipRejected` and withdrew. `72fdb41` split that out as `MissingLocalKey` and made both withdraw branches log. So it was the same underlying blackout reached through a different site, not a new race and not the split causing it. At `c8ec3fb` the twelve runs carry 25 `retained the verified set while unready` warnings — roughly two per run — so the condition occurred throughout and was handled every time, which the previous round's twelve could not claim because the race never fired in them. But one consequence is **not** resolved: `verify-m7-trust-expiry` fails intermittently on this branch — **0 of 4 at the branch base, 1 of 3 to 1 of 4 across every branch variant tried** — — improved to 14 of 18 by the signed-boundary attribution fix, but still not 8 of 8. **This branch is therefore not landable and M3-04 is not promotable until that is resolved.** The diagnosis, the fix and the remaining question are on M7-C83. Workspace evidence at `6297458`: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` (clean; the only warnings are the pre-existing vendored `h3` ones); `cargo test --workspace --locked -- --test-threads=4` with `TEST_REDIS_URL`/`TUNNEL_CATALOG_REDIS_URL` on the Docker Redis fixture (1312 passed, 0 failed, 52 ignored, 123 s); `cargo test -p tunnel-test-harness --locked` standalone (326 passed, 0 failed, 12 ignored, 10 s). The M7 rerun set was then widened to every gate `cluster.md` and `scripts/m7-harness-verify.sh` name for peer trust, membership or readiness — eighteen in all, adding `m7_deployment_spki_replacement`, `m7_readiness`, `m7_membership_persistence`, `m7_live_membership`, `m7_live_membership_boot_replacement`, `verify-m7-cluster`, `verify-m7-transport`, `verify-m7-admission`, `verify-m7-admission-framing` and `verify-m7-production` to the eight above. Seventeen pass; `verify-m7-trust-expiry` is the open regression recorded above. Per-run logs carrying each exit code are retained.<br><br>**Completion date withheld.** This row is not verified, so it carries no `Completed at`.<br><br>**Still not proven, and still named.** Server&rarr;client JSON-RPC request IDs (the fixture issues none; M3-13); `Origin` validation and the MCP authorization profile, including audience checks at the export (M3-11); `Last-Event-ID` resume of an interrupted legacy stream (M3-10); and concurrent colliding request IDs *through one shared backend process* - `streamable-binding` drives the shared backend for session separation, but the `correlation` case's colliding IDs and progress tokens still run on the stdio exports only (M3-13). The M7 defects M7-C80, M7-C82, M7-C84 and M7-C85 are unchanged; M7-C83's part 2 is answered by this row's fix and its part 1 (a focused back-to-back re-sign regression) stays open. | — |

| M3-09 | [ ] Close or bound the stdio export's process-tree residue: descendants that leave the child's process group. | planned | mcp | **Constraint** found in the M3-02 review. The stdio export kills the child's whole process group on every end of life, but a descendant that calls `setsid` or `setpgid`, or daemonizes with a double fork, is outside the group and survives. Group signalling is Unix-only. Options: a per-export cgroup or job object, a subreaper with descendant tracking, or a documented operator constraint that servers must not detach. Include a regression with a fixture that detaches. | — |
| M3-10 | [ ] Decide and test `Last-Event-ID` resume for the `mcp-2025-11-25` profile. | planned | mcp | The stdio bridge emits no SSE event IDs, so a legacy stream cannot resume. For an HTTP backend the header is allowlisted and forwarded, but resume is untested. Either implement bounded, principal-scoped resume with no replay across streams, or advertise that there is no resume and test the client's behaviour without it. | — |
| M3-11 | [ ] Implement the MCP HTTP authorization profile at the relay: protected-resource discovery, audience and scope checks, and strict `Origin` handling for browser-capable endpoints. | planned | mcp | Required by [mcp.md](mcp.md), Gateway boundaries. Today both profiles refuse `origin`, and the public route verifies only the tunnel bearer scope `http:invoke`. | — |
| M3-12 | [ ] Prove the MCP profiles for HTTP/2 consumers and on non-Unix device hosts. | planned | mcp | Both profiles accept consumer HTTP/2, but it is untested on the real path (as for gates 3–4). The stdio export's group kill and every end-to-end test are `cfg(unix)`; a Windows device needs its own process-tree containment and test run. | — |
| M3-13 | [ ] Exercise resources, prompts, `subscriptions/listen`, MRTR input requests, sampling and elicitation through both MCP export kinds. | planned | mcp | The bridge forwards these as raw JSON-RPC, but no fixture tool or test covers them. Extend `tunnel-mcp-fixture` and run them per profile; for 2026, `subscriptions/listen` on a per-request child. | — |
| M3-14 | [ ] Record an ingress exchange when a consumer disconnects before any response head. | planned | unassigned (defect found by the m3c6 worker) | **Defect.** The public route's `ingress_remote` exchange record is written by the task spawned after `forward_paused` returns a response head. A consumer that disconnects while a request is still dispatched and unanswered (an MCP client cancelling a `tools/call` whose first message has not arrived) drops that future, so relay-c records no exchange at all, while the owner correctly records `RESET(4005 CANCELLED)` and the device records the cancelled exchange. Cancellation itself is unaffected; the ingress diagnostics are simply missing, and `exchanges_recorded` under-counts. Observed in every `verify-m3-mcp-cloud-client` 2026 stdio cancellation case. **Remaining.** Record the exchange from a drop guard (or before the head), and add a regression that cancels before the head and requires the ingress record. | — |
| M3-15 | [ ] Decide what a consumer should see when a rotation freeze refuses a new MCP request. | planned | unassigned (found by the m3c6 worker) | **Constraint.** New stream admission is paused from QUIESCE to COMMIT (docs/protocol.md, "Quiesce admission"), so a POST that lands in a freeze is refused with a retryable `503 PEER_UNAVAILABLE` `not_dispatched` and a bounded `retry_after_ms`. rmcp 3.4.0 does not retry it, so with a 6 s rotation policy an MCP client sees sporadic 503s. The same body is also the relay's answer to every other owner-not-ready condition (`http.rs` `retryable_peer_failure_response`: no active carrier, an unfenced owner, an unknown owner write), so a consumer cannot tell a scheduled freeze from a fault state from the answer alone. `verify-m3-mcp-cloud-client` therefore resends such a refusal only while its own watch on the connector's rotation phase says a rotation is frozen, with a cap derived from the rotation handshake budget at the relay's smallest hint, and its validator requires every POST and standalone-GET refusal to have coincided with a freeze. A refusal in the head gap before the connector sees `ROTATE_QUIESCE` is reported with the connector's phase and rotation count rather than failing namelessly. A new device session also makes an ingress that still resolves the previous owner token answer `PEER_UNAVAILABLE` with `execution: unknown` until it re-resolves. **Remaining.** Decide between queueing the OPEN until COMMIT, advertising `Retry-After` for MCP consumers, or documenting the retry contract for gateways; consider distinguishing the scheduled-freeze refusal from the fault refusals so a consumer can retry only the safe one; and test the chosen behaviour. | — |
| M3-16 | [ ] Decide whether a revoked grant should end the device-side MCP session, not only make it unreachable. | planned | mcp (found by the m3c8 worker) | **Constraint** observed by `verify-m3-mcp-isolation`. Revoking a consumer grant fences admission at the relay within ~10 ms and withdraws the admitted exchange within ~500 ms, but the device never learns: the `mcp-2025-11-25` session and its stdio child survive, holding one of the export's `max_children` slots. Nothing is dispatched to it — the relay refuses everything the revoked principal sends, including its DELETE — so this is a resource-retention question, not an authorization hole, and the retention is now bounded on both sides: the session ends at `session_idle_seconds` (default 600 s), and in any case when the connector's handler registry goes away, so it never outlives the device session. What remains open is the slot held while that device session lives. Decide between (a) leaving it to idle expiry and documenting the retention, (b) shortening the idle timeout for sessions whose grant revision has advanced, or (c) giving the connector a control message that ends the sessions of a revoked grant. | — |
| M3-17 | [ ] Pin an MCP conformance suite or a non-Rust MCP SDK, or record that neither is in M3's scope. | planned | mcp (split out of M3-01) | **Owner for a statement M3-01 could no longer carry.** M3-01 closed with "no conformance suite or non-Rust SDK is pinned", deferring it to M3-03; M3-03 is now verified local without pinning either, so the statement had no owner. Both MCP profiles are proven only against the pinned rmcp 3.4.0 client and the deterministic `tunnel-mcp-fixture` server, so nothing here shows an independent implementation interoperating. Decide between pinning a published conformance suite, pinning one non-Rust SDK as a second client, or recording that single-implementation evidence is M3's deliberate scope and moving interoperability to a later milestone. Whichever is chosen, state it in [mcp.md](mcp.md) rather than leaving it implicit. | — |

| M4-07 | [ ] Settle the filesystem confinement and capability contract, and implement the pure core it defines. | implemented awaiting verification | filesystem (m4c1 worker) | **Implementation gate 1 of [filesystem-api.md](filesystem-api.md#implementation-gates).** The document was substantive on the wire, endpoint and SDK surface but settled none of the device-side confinement or authorization rules, so it is amended with a normative [confinement and capability model](filesystem-api.md#confinement-and-capability-model), a numbered six-gate list and a [Pinned in code (gate 1)](filesystem-api.md#pinned-in-code-gate-1) section, and `crates/tunnel-fs-core` implements exactly that: the virtual path namespace, the read/write/list/delete capability model with its 24 enforced primitives and 17 derived operations, the negotiated limits, the payload-free error vocabulary and the `agent-tunnel.fs.v1` descriptor. Dependency-free, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, no I/O, no clock, no socket, no network in tests. **Evidence at `1daec97`:** 66 crate tests green — every refused path class refused by its own rule (**57** corpus cases, with a coverage assertion that every `PathRule` is exercised somewhere), every limit exact at its ceiling and refused one beyond and at zero and at `u64::MAX` (14 fields each), default deny and the advertise-only-what-is-permitted invariant exhaustive over all 16 grants × 128 feature sets, no error rendering containing a path across a **118**-value sweep that is now pinned to the exact vocabulary size, and the emitted descriptor reproducing `docs/contracts/filesystem-capabilities.example.json` byte for byte; generative coverage from a seeded dependency-free generator (20,000 assembled paths and 20,000 arbitrary-scalar paths, **both with acceptance and rejection floors asserted**, 5,000 parent-chain walks, 10,000 random grants); plus `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, strict locked workspace Clippy, and the full workspace suite. **Red-then-green:** 17 guards at the first revision plus 9 more for the review fixes, each deleted in turn, re-run red, and restored. **Remaining before this row can be verified:** an independent review of the amended contract, and confirmation that gate 2 can be built against these types without widening them. **Explicitly not covered:** everything in gate 2 — symlinks, hard links and `st_nlink`, mount points, special files, file identity and every TOCTOU race, none of which a crate that never opens a file can prove — plus all four two-spellings-one-file classes (ASCII case, Unicode case pairs, NFC/NFD and NTFS 8.3 short names), non-UTF-8 input (the codec's gate), the deliberately accepted C1 controls and bidirectional overrides, 9P encoding and `msize` framing, deadlines and clocks, and quota accounting. | — |
| M4-08 | [ ] Implement the OS-confined resolver: anchored traversal, symlink and hard-link policy, file identity, mount points and special files, and TOCTOU. | implemented awaiting verification | filesystem (m4c2 worker) | **Implementation gate 2 of [filesystem-api.md](filesystem-api.md#implementation-gates).** `crates/tunnel-fs-host` resolves a validated virtual path to an open descriptor by per-component anchored traversal from a retained root descriptor, and every later decision is taken on that descriptor. Depends only on `tunnel-fs-core` and on `rustix`, already in the tree for M3; its `fs` feature is the safe wrapper the no-`unsafe` rule requires, so **no new third-party crate** is added. The choices it pins are recorded under [Pinned in code (gate 2)](filesystem-api.md#pinned-in-code-gate-2). **Evidence at `840c810`:** 48 tests green with the `race-window-hook` feature (45 without it) and one `#[ignore]`d, plus cross-target `cargo clippy -p tunnel-fs-host --all-targets --locked --target ... -- -D warnings` clean in all four states that exist: `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, each both with and without the feature. (Round 1 checked Windows only in its default state, and the feature-enabled Windows build did fail: the race-window test file was gated on the feature but not on `unix`, so it reached for a resolver that does not exist there. It is now gated on both.) Those cross-checks stand in for hosted CI, which is billing-blocked, and a cross-target `clippy` is a type-check, never a test run — symlink escapes (absolute, relative, chained 3-deep, 40-link chain above the 32-hop budget, cycle, dangling, intermediate-component, re-rooted to a real inside file, and an absolute target anchored at the root rather than at the link's own directory), a directory link swapped for a real directory and back between resolutions, the `st_nlink` write refusal on `OpenWrite`/`OpenTruncate`/`SetattrSize` with reads and `SetattrMode`/`SetattrTimes` unaffected and content verified unchanged after each refusal, `Tlink` and the symlink primitives refused without their features, a FIFO and a unix socket refused with `ENOTSUP` before being opened, a **real mount point** (an unprivileged `hdiutil` disk image attached inside the export) refused `EXDEV`, identity preserved across a rename and distinguishing a hard link from an identical-content copy, the three testable alias classes each decided by host identity, a 32-component path at the limit resolving and one beyond refused by gate 1, and a racing-thread TOCTOU test (772–820 observed swaps, both outcomes seen, no descriptor outside the root in 20,000 resolutions); plus `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, strict locked workspace Clippy, and the full workspace suite with Redis. **Red-then-green, re-measured after an earlier round reported three false REDs that were really compile failures:** 25 deletions, each applied, re-run under the feature and restored, by [`scripts/fs-guard-deletion.py`](../scripts/fs-guard-deletion.py) — committed with the evidence, so the safeguard that makes it trustworthy is readable rather than asserted: its `run_tests` refuses to call a build that did not compile a red test, and the script refuses to start against a dirty working tree, which is how the first round's false REDs arose. 16 of the 25 turn a test red: **12 single deletions** — the symlink-feature refusal, the 32-hop budget, absolute re-rooting, the `..` clamp, the link-target component bound, the trailing-separator rule, the uniform race fold, the **file** post-open identity comparison, the hard-link write refusal, its directory exclusion, the grant check and the errno-by-meaning translation — and **four combinations**: the two special-file checks as a pair, the three mount-boundary checks as a triple, and each `O_NOFOLLOW` with its sibling identity check. **Only the file identity comparison is individually load-bearing among the four window guards:** each `O_NOFOLLOW` and its sibling identity check mask one another and are proven in pairs, and the three mount-boundary checks mask one another and are proven as a triple. `EINTR` retry has no test at all, and the Linux post-open identity check has no deletion evidence, because the hook has no call site there. **Remaining before this row can be verified:** an independent review, a run on Linux to exercise the `openat2` path, and confirmation that gate 3's codec can be built on these types. **Explicitly not covered:** the Linux `openat2` path (type-checked for the Linux target from this Mac, never executed — macOS is the only host available, and full-workspace cross-target checking is blocked by a missing C cross-toolchain, so the cross-checks cover this crate and its dependency); a real device node (`mknod` needs privilege, so the case is `#[ignore]`d with that reason rather than a passing skip, and the refusal rests on an exhaustive kind decision plus the demonstrated FIFO and socket cases); NTFS 8.3 short names, closed by **declaring filesystem exports unsupported on Windows** rather than by test; and `Treadlink`/`Tsymlink`/`Tlink` execution, enumeration, create, unlink, mkdir, non-size `Tsetattr`, 9P framing, deadlines and quota accounting, which are gates 3 to 5. | — |

## Updating rules

1. Add a stable-ID task when a defect, implementation slice, or missing test is
   discovered, before implementing it. Put the current owner and the exact
   acceptance scope in the same change.
2. Record the exact command, fixture, revision, and evidence document for every
   verified-local status. A component test cannot close a cross-relay,
   process-lifecycle, Redis-authority, adapter, release-artifact, or VM task.
3. After networking, authorization, fixture, dependency, or protocol changes,
   demote affected tested rows to implemented awaiting verification until the
   required commands are rerun. Do not promote a stale result.
4. Keep M1/M2 historical local results tied to their stated date and scope.
   Hosted CI, production readiness, and alpha status require separate evidence
   and must never be inferred from those local results.
5. Use in progress only for work actively being implemented or run. Use
   blocked only when a named prerequisite or external dependency prevents
   progress; record that blocker in the acceptance column.
6. A milestone checklist is complete only when all required rows are verified
   at their declared scope and its linked evidence is current. Keep edge-case
   coverage in [m7-edge-cases.md](m7-edge-cases.md) and link the relevant stable
   task IDs here.

| 2026-09-16T16:30:00+10:00 | M3-04; M3-16 | review round 1 fixes; still implemented awaiting verification | Four majors closed. The owner no longer trusts a relayed principal binding: it re-derives one from the identifiers it authorized for itself and resets `HTTP_INVALID_HEAD` `not_dispatched` on a mismatch or an absent binding, which matters because the digest is unkeyed over catalog identifiers. The ingress refusal that the unkeyed design rests on now has a relay unit test and a `binding-forgery` gate case (two profiles × three routes × single and repeated header: twelve refusals, twelve ingress rejections, zero device dispatch). A legacy session's detached pump no longer outlives its export: dropping an export, `McpExport::shutdown`, or dropping the connector's `HttpHandlers` ends every open session and kills each child's process group, and the gate now requires no export child to survive the connector stop. The Streamable HTTP export refuses a new session when its table or the principal's share is full instead of evicting another principal's live session, which was a cross-principal denial channel, and only the exchange that opened a session binds it. Minors: a bare transport close is no longer reported as a typed interruption; indistinguishability is compared field for field on POST, GET and DELETE against an unknown-session control; the replay detector settles on a device exchange record or the device session ending instead of a sleep, and the standalone streams are opened before either call; the shared-backend correlation gap is recorded as not covered; and the infallible hex-to-header conversion no longer maps to a 500. |

Its capacity refusal is counted in the export's `rejected`. `HttpHandlers` lost its `Clone` derive: its `Drop` ends every protocol session its exports hold, which a second copy would trigger early. The owner compares the binding header name ASCII-case-insensitively. The gate's OPEN journal bound is now derived rather than tuned: two adjacent correlation bursts may be unreclaimed at once, so `CORRELATION_CALLS * 4` = 24, still far under the 128 tracked entries. |

Ten minors also closed: `Display` now derives its code from `code()` so the two length rules read `ENAMETOOLONG` rather than a hardcoded `EINVAL`; the arbitrary-scalar generator forced a leading `/` on half its seeds, because a random first scalar is `/` with probability 1 in 0x110000 and its acceptance arm had been accepting 0 of 20,000 - both generators now assert acceptance and rejection floors; `DEVICE_OFFLINE` maps to 1012 and `ABORTED` to no close code at all, since cancelling one operation must not close a session carrying other tags; `maxMessageBytes` no longer presents as `EFBIG`, because an over-`msize` frame is a 1002 framing violation and never an `Rlerror`; a separator in a joined component has its own rule instead of being misreported as an empty component; the refusal table records non-UTF-8 as the codec's gate and the acceptance of C1 controls, bidirectional overrides and U+2028/U+2029 as a deliberate narrowing; the checking order is stated and the table marked unordered; an empty grant is recorded as answering 403 at discovery rather than serving an empty operation list; and read+list is named as the read-only profile, since `read` alone has no `stat` for a Files SDK head or exists call. **Evidence counts in the row and the first history entry were wrong and are corrected here rather than by editing them:** the escape corpus had 50 entries, not 46, and now has 57; the error sweep rendered 117 values, not 88, and now renders 118. Both counts are pinned by assertions so they cannot drift again. 66 tests green; 9 further guards deleted and re-run red before restoring. No verification status changed. |

The export root is the only path ever opened by name, once, at construction; every component after it is opened from its parent's descriptor, and the kind, the identity and the link count are read with `fstat` on the descriptor that was opened rather than with a second look at the name. Re-measured with a harness that refuses to report a failed build: **only the file post-open identity comparison is individually load-bearing** among the four window guards; each `O_NOFOLLOW` and its sibling identity check mask one another and are proven in pairs, and the three mount-boundary checks are proven as a triple. The row and the gate-2 section now say that instead of "proven by deletion" for `O_NOFOLLOW`, and the Linux post-open check is recorded as having no deletion evidence at all. **The Linux path had four real gaps:** `openat2` decided mount crossing on the final `st_dev` only, so a bind mount of an outside directory under a mount inside the root would have been refused by the per-component walk and permitted by the kernel path — it now carries `RESOLVE_NO_XDEV | RESOLVE_NO_MAGICLINKS`; `EAGAIN` from the kernel's own rename detection fell through to `EINVAL` and is now the race answer; `open_directory` returned an `O_PATH` descriptor, which answers `EBADF` to `getdents`, so directories are upgraded too; and `/proc/self/fd` was a silent hard dependency whose absence would have reported every read as a missing file, so it is probed once and the per-component walk is used without it. Four more fixes apply on every host: `EINTR` is retried rather than reported as `EINVAL` (the one guard with no test, and it is named as such); the device boundary is decided before each open as well as after, so another filesystem's open handler never runs; a link target ending in `/` requires a directory, as POSIX does, instead of silently resolving to a regular file; and `ELOOP`/`ENOTDIR` from the two component opens fold into the uniform `ENOENT`, so a racer cannot learn which swap it won — the contract had asserted that uniformity while the code gave three different answers. **Test honesty:** the mount-point and socket cases printed a skip and passed, which left the same green count whether they ran or not; both are hard failures now, the device node is `#[ignore]`d with its reason, and the alias tests call `ExportRoot::same_file` instead of re-implementing it, so the shipped decision is what they exercise. 48 tests green under the feature, 45 without, one ignored. The timestamp above is this machine's clock, which still reads earlier than the two M4-07 entries; treat the order in this document, not the clock, as the sequence. No verification status changed. |
